2026-10-30T04:59:21.0476211Z   QWEN_COMMIT_MESSAGE: [cluster:firmus] [nodes:2] [prefill-pp4] Serve the round-4 bundle again on its image with a 600-call endurance gate for the MegaMoE split variant and without the FA3 pipe screen

Run 40991744289 (build and serving of a792523) built and archived the image
(sha256 db6eeb49...9fe5, 12297687040 bytes, source a792523, SGLang fc27567).
Its Slurm job 27484 then timed out with no score.

What the in-job selections adopted:
- Prefill MegaMoE four-stage-lean-split (in-job median 2480 us against lean
  2546 and control 2646).
- Prefill FA3 row n192-direct-o-varlen-pipe.
- Decode MoE input fused.
- Decode QKV BLOCK_M 192 x N80.
- The Decode FA3 two-heads rows did not win.
- The KV read probe measured 2.91 / 3.04 / 3.25 TB/s at 128 / 256 / 512 B.

What failed: AIPerf warmup returned 77 requests. All three Prefill workers
stopped issuing work about 13 microbatches into serving (11:55:13-16), within
2 s of each other. Their KV pools were nearly full (2944 of 131072 tokens
free, 10 in flight). Decode then waited on about 50 transfer requests per
rank, and the scheduler watchdog fired after 300 s. No CUDA error appears,
and Prefill and Decode GPU power fell to idle within seconds.

Suspects: the two Prefill kernels that served for the first time.
- MegaMoE lean-split has no timeout trap, so a stuck call would also stay
  silent.
- The FA3 pipe row was reviewed for barrier counts, tile counter reset and
  empty tiles, and no hang path was found.
The in-job selections make only about 30 calls per GPU, so they cannot catch
a hang after a few hundred calls. Genkai endurance probes are queued until
tomorrow, so this run splits the two suspects on Firmus itself:

1. FA3 pipe screen removed from the PP4 Prefill chain (42461e9 cherry-picked
   as 5612161). The pinned n192-direct-o-varlen row serves. The pipe row stays
   in the image and in prefill-candidates.json. It costs at most 0.2-0.35 ms
   per period, so leaving it out is cheap and removes one suspect.

2. MegaMoE endurance gate (c8921b4 cherry-picked as f0e63f3, without the
   SPLIT_LDSM host patch, which would need a rebuild).
   - After the selection, one srun step per Prefill worker runs in parallel
     with a 90 s timeout.
   - It recomputes the winner. If the winner has not served before (anything
     other than control, four-stage or lean), it runs that winner for 600
     back-to-back EP1 calls per GPU, rotating the balanced, hot and edges
     routings. It then checks the last output per routing bit for bit against
     control.
   - The aggregate adopts the winner only if all twelve GPUs finished and
     matched. Otherwise it serves the fastest served variant (lean served
     12k+ batches in 40964714831) and records why.
   - Either outcome locates the hang. If the gate stalls, lean serves and
     split is the cause. If the gate passes and serving still stops, the cause
     is split under serving conditions.

3. Pins: configs/firmus-prefill-pp4.json pins the image of 40991744289, and
   check-firmus-pp4-cpu.py PINNED_IMAGE follows. Nothing that goes into the
   image changed since a792523. A test slice in
   test_prefill_megamoe_selection.py is adjusted to the overlay list without
   SPLIT_LDSM.

Expected, if split passes: Prefill -1.5..-2.6% from split, plus the Decode
MoE input and BLOCK_M 192 tiles. The round-3 serving model gives about
9,800-10,000 at c232.

Timeline: Prefill-GPU chain (selection and gate) about 2-2.7 min, inside the
Decode-GPU chain of about 4-4.5 min. READY at about 480-500 s and Slurm
elapsed about 14:00-14:30, more than 2 minutes inside the limit.
No profile pass is requested in this message.

Validation: full pytest suite exit 0; test_decode_step_overlays.py separately;
ruff clean; bash -n on the sbatch.

- The endurance gate result in prefill-megamoe selection.json (choices per

- Whether serving passes the 13th microbatch.
